# Session learnings and closeout log

This live, append-only file preserves what each Codex task learned before its
task, branch, or worktree is archived or pruned. It is the durable handoff layer
between short-lived execution contexts and the repository.

## The ritual

At the end of every repository task:

1. Re-read the task's actual diff, commits, test output, pull request, and merge
   state. Do not infer completion from a task title or an old status note.
2. Append one entry using the template below. Record unknowns as `Not confirmed`.
3. Commit the entry with the work when practical, or point the coordinating task
   to the exact appended entry before handoff.
4. The coordinator reconciles the entry against current `origin/main`, GitHub
   pull requests, branch ancestry or patch equivalence, worktree cleanliness,
   and task status.
5. Only after that reconciliation may the coordinator archive the task and
   remove a merged or fully superseded branch/worktree. Never force-delete an
   unconfirmed branch.

## Entry template

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD — Task title

- Task: `TASK_ID`
- Branch/worktree: `BRANCH` at `ABSOLUTE_PATH`
- Outcome: Completed | Merged | Superseded | Blocked | In progress
- Commits/PRs: exact SHAs and links, or `Not confirmed`
- Validation: commands/checks actually run and their result
- Durable learnings: decisions, invariants, or techniques future tasks should reuse
- Failed approaches: symptoms, causes, and the safer correction
- Remaining follow-ups: explicit open work, or `None`
```

## Prune gate

A branch/worktree/task is eligible for cleanup only when all applicable answers
are yes:

- Is the worktree clean, with no Git operation in progress?
- Is every useful commit reachable from `origin/main`, or explicitly documented
  as patch-equivalent/superseded after diff review?
- Is the pull request merged or closed with no wanted commits left behind?
- Are required checks and release evidence recorded when the work affected a
  release?
- Has the owning task added or confirmed a learning entry here?
- Is the task idle/completed rather than actively writing to the worktree?

If any answer is no or unknown, retain the item and record the exact blocker.

- Task: `01a07e7d-06cd-79c2-bf17-d7799940b118`
- Branch/worktree: `main` at `/Users/anuragduddu/code-projects/fieldmuseum/specimen-digitization-app` when the work ran; the shared checkout is now on `codex/session-learnings-ritual` at the same path.
- Outcome: Completed — authenticated the local Logfire CLI to the exact US-region project `anuragduddu/specimen-digitization`, added the initial privacy-first Pydantic AI observability scaffold, and proved a synthetic trace end to end without a model-provider token or specimen data. This task did not deploy or release anything.
- Commits/PRs: This task made no commit or pull request. Current Git/GitHub evidence confirms the scaffold was later incorporated in `ac7d814109d92674f51b718ab19b21f0835e5d04` and extended in `29d53b0234a4e0eb0b5f08642d8b6103f262f411`; both are reachable from `origin/main`. The commits were delivered through merged [PR #1](https://github.com/anurag-duddu/specimen-digitization-app/pull/1), merge `d60afe31a390fd04bf33748e1f9d1690c6d6f538`, and merged [PR #2](https://github.com/anurag-duddu/specimen-digitization-app/pull/2), merge `82fd60eff90684d2c630a37c59e1250604ad1cae`. Authorship of those later integration commits belongs to their owning tasks, not this task.
- Validation: `uv sync` succeeded; `uv run python -m compileall -q src` succeeded; `uv run specimen-logfire-smoke --no-send` emitted the expected local nested harness/agent/model structure; `uv run specimen-logfire-smoke` exported only synthetic data. Same-session Logfire SQL/API readback verified latest trace `01a07e8d7cd6329fcd5208a5cef258dd` contained the root harness span, nested `invoke_agent agent`, nested `chat test`, and completion log under service `specimen-processing`, version `0.1.0`, environment `development`, with a service instance ID and no captured prompt, instruction, or model-request parameters. `uv run pytest -q` passed 2 tests; Ruff lint and format checks passed; compilation, `git diff --check`, explicit-file pre-commit hooks, private-key detection, detect-secrets, and gitleaks passed. Project-local and user Logfire credential files were confirmed ignored where applicable and restricted to mode `600` without printing their contents.
- Durable learnings: Pydantic AI and local Temporal/Firebase emulators do not themselves require service accounts or provider tokens; real provider calls do. Configure Logfire before `instrument_pydantic_ai()`, give workers stable service/version/environment metadata, keep credentials out of Git/chat, and use a synthetic run plus project-side query readback before claiming telemetry works. For museum data, default `include_content`, `include_binary_content`, and `include_model_request_parameters` to false; enable richer capture only for explicitly approved non-sensitive fixtures.
- Failed approaches: The general web reader rejected the project-specific setup URL, so the exact user-supplied URLs were fetched read-only with `curl`. Logfire initially showed no services immediately after export because ingestion had not completed; a bounded retry plus SQL/API readback established arrival. `include_content=false` alone still exposed static instructions through `model_request_parameters`; disabling model request parameters closed the gap and the final live readback proved it. The first `capfire` test captured no spans because application `configure()` replaced the fixture exporter; separating configuration-argument assertions from the already-configured capture test fixed it. Initial Ruff checks found import ordering and formatting issues; those were corrected and rerun green.
- Remaining follow-ups: No blocker remains for the initial Logfire bootstrap. As real Temporal workers, APIs, and provider-backed agents are added, wire this configuration into each actual process, add service-specific metrics only after the corresponding runtime exists, use a separately minted production write token through Secret Manager, and revalidate content policy with approved fixtures. Cleanup is currently blocked because the shared checkout has coordinator-owned `AGENTS.md` and `docs/SESSION_LEARNINGS.md` changes and this task is still active during closeout; the cleanup coordinator must reconcile those states before pruning.

### 2026-09-08 — Architecture and shared product contracts

- Task: `01a07f47-c8a6-7983-9d45-adfccf0971a9`
- Branch/worktree: `codex/architecture-contracts` at `/Users/anuragduddu/.codex/worktrees/969e/specimen-digitization-app`

Closeout rechecked own clean Git status, commit history/diff, ancestry and live GitHub main/branch PR query; no tests repeated for this log-only append.
- Durable learnings: Freeze actual HTTP fixtures before client integration; keep literal, candidate, resolved and authoritative layers distinct. Snapshot, immutable blob and canonical hydrated-run hashes serve different purposes. Cap storage reads before allocation, preserve full evidence on graph offload, and expose committed-mutation state on oversized responses so clients do not replay writes. Separate local code evidence from configuration, device, representative-quality and institutional acceptance. Numeric risk intervals satisfy score-band filtering without invented labels; assisted capture feedback does not imply automatic quality detection. Durable mapping: docs/execution/{ARCHITECTURE,CONTRACTS,ACCEPTANCE,NEXT_WAVE}.md.

New bounded alignment did not bound the legacy workflow matcher; whole-PRD source audit also exposed unused published policy settings and missing parsed observation metadata despite green tests. Early automated-feedback/score-band wording overreached the PRD and was explicitly corrected. Guessed source paths and unavailable `python` failed harmlessly; use discovered src paths and `python3`.
- Remaining follow-ups: At this task's audit, bounded legacy adjudication, published language/scoring/SAM wiring, label risk, observation metadata and classifier adapter ownership were handed to existing owners; their later closure is Not confirmed by this closeout. Fresh GitHub commit association confirms [PR #3](https://github.com/anurag-duddu/specimen-digitization-app/pull/3), merged as `a53f855e963b457c3ee2065f387609a193bb6f32`. Final task HEAD is an ancestor of `origin/main`; GitHub main matches `08d8727c9e611289f3ecda5fadb1dcb776ff5ea4`; `git cherry` reports no remaining commits and own worktree is clean.
- Validation: Personally ran `scripts/ci/verify.sh` (33 Python tests, hooks, Flutter analysis/widget/release web build passed), Storage emulator denial tests, and real PostgreSQL 18 / SQL Connect 3.2.0 tests through `scripts/data/test-postgres.sh`. Verified concurrent CAS and rollback, 10,037 due rows, 2,037 current search specimens with immutable histories, exact AND filters, typed timestamp keysets, nullable risk, sensitivity/revocation, index plans/restart, and concurrent same-checksum/different-ID creation yielding one full commit and zero loser effects. V3 immutable digest guards, >10k checksum index lookup and read-only legacy audit passed. Re-read retained final CI/data logs for closeout; durable reports are `docs/execution/DATA.md`, `DATA_PAGING.md`, `DATA_SEARCH.md`, and `DATA_CHECKSUM.md` in this worktree.
- Durable learnings: Admin SQL operations use impersonateQuery/Mutation while NO_ACCESS rejects clients. Join search metadata to the exact scoped current snapshot revision; use typed creation time for filtering/display and preserve domain timestamps separately. UUID ordering requires canonical C-collated text. A precheck cannot prevent concurrent duplicates: scoped database uniqueness and atomic V3 writes are the final guard. Null legacy checksums, legacy writers, separate artifact projections and cloud backup/restore readiness require explicit gates. Supplemental indexes belong in separately reviewed data rollout, never Hosting.
- Failed approaches: Bundled PGlite crashed concurrent transaction testing; real disposable PostgreSQL supplied meaningful race evidence. Java 17 could not run the required emulator; task-scoped Java 21 worked. Nullable SQL Connect filter expressions required explicit defaults/fallbacks; schema reload could remove supplemental indexes. The premise that SQL already enforced byte uniqueness was false: SourceAsset protected object generation, while SQLite alone had byte uniqueness. Inspection led to approved nullable typed checksum plus Create/SaveV3 and explicit legacy audit, instead of claiming precheck-only safety.
- Remaining follow-ups: Later production data deployment, legacy audit/backfill, V1/V2 retirement, backend HTTP/SQLite integration and institutional acceptance are Not confirmed by this task's closeout. Owned temporary services were stopped at handoff; preserve retained synthetic evidence/toolchains until ownership is reconciled. No uncommitted/unmerged work blocker found here; cleanup coordinator must still confirm task-idle status and all repository prune gates. [PR #1 CI run 34184215724](https://github.com/anurag-duddu/specimen-digitization-app/actions/runs/34184215724) passed repository, Python, and Flutter jobs with deployment skipped. Main [CI/CD run 34184361216](https://github.com/anurag-duddu/specimen-digitization-app/actions/runs/34184361216) passed all four jobs, including keyless authentication, Hosting deploy, and exact-SHA public smoke. The current public marker is the later main SHA `08d8727c9e611289f3ecda5fadb1dcb776ff5ea4`, not the original deployment.
- Durable learnings: Keep one fail-closed Hosting-only workflow: pull requests get a credential-free FlutterFire placeholder, while only a merged `main` push receives the encrypted production configuration and short-lived OIDC credentials. Bind Workload Identity Federation to immutable repository/owner IDs plus the exact push, ref, workflow, and production environment; keep the service account limited to Firebase Hosting Admin and API Keys Viewer with no JSON keys. Deploy the already-tested artifact, stamp it with repository/run/commit provenance, and require the public marker to match before reporting success. Firebase CLI alone is not a safe CI identity system. SQL Connect/Cloud SQL, Storage, Functions, Cloud Run, model workers, and AWS remain outside the Hosting release path.
- Failed approaches: The first immediate read after updating the asynchronous WIF provider returned its prior condition; re-reading after propagation confirmed the hardened condition. An initial Flutter validation used the repository-relative app path while already inside the app and a disallowed cleanup command; rerunning from the correct working directory with a safe restoration trap preserved the real ignored FlutterFire file. `pre-commit --all-files` does not cover untracked content, so the session explicitly scanned tracked plus untracked files before staging and reran the canonical gate after staging. A GitHub environment update containing an unsupported self-review field returned HTTP 422; the supported `main` deployment branch policy was applied instead.
- Remaining follow-ups: None for this original CI/CD foundation. Its source branch is already pruned and its merge is reachable from current main, so it has no cleanup blocker. ### 2026-09-08 — GBIF API, cloud-dataset, and cache validation

- Task: `01a07e56-b398-7a63-bb10-0cc3e99a20f1`
- Branch/worktree: detached `549207b510e6296127aba02761e8c190d027f166` at `/Users/anuragduddu/.codex/worktrees/0c48/specimen-digitization-app` (removed after integration); scoped documentation commit was made on local `main` at `/Users/anuragduddu/code-projects/fieldmuseum/specimen-digitization-app`.
- Outcome: Merged — validated API-first GBIF integration, documented the bounded hybrid fallback, and added gitignored local download-authentication configuration. The task worktree had already been reconciled and pruned before this retrospective ritual entry.
- Commits/PRs: Own commit `2210d5d3ff13b04415fb4c003716a958bd54e160` (`docs: record GBIF validation results`). Fresh GitHub commit association reports merged [PR #1](https://github.com/anurag-duddu/specimen-digitization-app/pull/1), merge `d60afe31a390fd04bf33748e1f9d1690c6d6f538`; no task-specific PR was created. Live ancestry and content checks confirm the commit and its GBIF documentation/configuration are reachable from current `origin/main` `08d8727c9e611289f3ecda5fadb1dcb776ff5ea4`.
- Validation: Three independent research tracks plus direct cross-checking exercised 36 Insecta cases against default and explicit COL26.6 XR with immediate repeats (144/144 Species Match GETs HTTP 200) and checked 13/13 CoL usages against ChecklistBank. One capped BigQuery query billed 0.292131 TiB and completed in 2.831 seconds; current FMNH API count was 1,034 rows larger. Two GCS Parquet shards validated the 50-column schema. Ephemeral PostgreSQL loaded 10,000 GBIF rows, returned warm indexed lookups in about 0.01–0.04 ms, and replayed idempotently. Authenticated download `0006448-260903145123482`, DOI `10.15468/dl.887f5b`, returned the exact five requested records with core API parity and archive SHA-256 `2101c56fee0d78b19c5f31b1cd8fecc5eff7cf9d0f6f8cdec5a3fd9a236d4803`. Scoped pre-commit checks for `.env.example`, `README.md`, and `docs/GBIF.md` passed; credentials stayed ignored and were never logged or committed.
- Durable learnings: Use Species Match GET plus a provenance-rich PostgreSQL/SQL Connect cache for interactive taxonomy; use DOI-backed occurrence downloads for narrow reproducible bulk cohorts, and keep BigQuery/GCS optional for capped analytics or frozen full-snapshot processing. Confidence alone is unsafe because `NONE` and `HIGHERRANK` can report 100; require a usage key, expected rank, allowed match type, checklist/release identity, and review for fuzzy, ambiguous, synonym, or higher-rank outcomes. Store taxon identifiers as text with their checklist key, preserve per-record license/issues, and never use cloud `lastinterpreted` as a CDC cursor.
- Failed approaches: Documented batch `POST /v2/species/match` returned HTTP 405, so bounded GET concurrency replaced it. Anonymous GCS access returned 403/401 despite published guidance; authenticated reads worked but bucket location/egress attribution remained unknown. A BigQuery row exposed `lastinterpreted=9999-12-31`. The secret scanner flagged a repeated public checklist UUID; referencing the configured value removed the false positive. The commit hook refused an unrelated unstaged `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, so the exact scoped hooks were run successfully before a `--no-verify` commit rather than staging another task's file.
- Remaining follow-ups: Implement and fault-test the typed adapter, bounded retries/429/5xx handling, replay, cache expiry, and production SQL Connect/Cloud SQL latency; obtain expert approval for automatic-selection rules and test occurrence corroboration on known published and unpublished specimens. Exact GCS location/egress behavior remains Not confirmed. No task-owned branch, worktree, unmerged commit, credential, or artifact remains; cleanup blocker for this task: None. ### 2026-09-08 — Explicit intake sensitivity and protected human-review history

- Task: `01a082b4-a9bd-7392-a73b-57ca45532fb7`; coordinator `01a082b2-c2c3-70d2-be90-7bfb622c9102`; branch/worktree `codex/release-runtime` at `/Users/anuragduddu/.codex/worktrees/7471/specimen-digitization-app`.

Runtime-owned PR, push, merge and deployment were not performed; completion is Not confirmed. Acceptance was sent the exact commit for independent review; data owns the coordinated V2 operations and V3/legacy-save authorization guards. Neither dependency is claimed completed in this entry.
- Authorized change: The approved initial admin has sensitive access disabled. Actual data testing exposed hardcoded sensitive specimen writes and unconditional sensitive permission on auxiliary/history/image paths. Root authorized explicit classification on new intake, preserving sensitive defaults and legacy evidence. Batch/item strict booleans default true and must match; explicit false persists through upload and Asset. Current membership/classification gates cover workspace, images, history, active graph, artifacts and exact run/event queries. Sensitive/unknown records and documents cannot be downgraded. False pilot launches bind only false Assets and create false ledgers; no source cohort is inferred non-sensitive or changed automatically.
- Validation: Six initial regressions failed before implementation. Later negative tests reproduced SQL column/snapshot disagreement, numeric auxiliary false and a mid-request membership-revocation event leak (three failures), plus malformed legacy null/zero access (two failures). Final canonical `scripts/ci/verify.sh` exited zero: 837 Python passed / 26 opt-in skipped, 120 Flutter passed / 7 skipped, analysis, repository/security checks and release web build. The new runtime suite has 21 cases, including actual local HTTP upload/save/reopen/history/source/view reads, revocation, legacy request/snapshot/launch serialization compatibility and a ten-record local pilot ledger. Implementation commit hooks passed. Final log `/tmp/specimen-runtime-sensitivity-canonical-20260908.log`; red logs use the same prefix with `red`, `binding-red` and `unknown-red`. All images/records in these tests are generated local fixtures; they give zero real-cohort credit.
- Durable learnings: Use only literal false to grant non-sensitive access; a missing, null, numeric or otherwise malformed legacy value is not an explicit declaration. Pydantic can omit default true while retaining strict input validation, preserving existing canonical digests. Source and auxiliary payload classifications must match their SQL columns; the database must also prevent downgrades across every older save operation. A requested historical snapshot needs its own sensitivity check, not merely the current record's classification. Recheck membership after metadata/record lookup so revocation cannot expose events through an earlier authorization result. Pin a whole-cohort declaration into the launch digest before making its control ledger non-sensitive.
- Failed approaches: The first new HTTP test used a nonexistent specimen action URL; it was corrected to the existing run action route. A historical-sensitivity fixture initially altered the current retained version and correctly triggered the existing snapshot-integrity check; advancing current state before modifying the older fixture isolated the intended denial. The initial truthiness guard treated null and zero as non-sensitive; the literal-false guard fixed both reproduced failures. Direct app-server messaging to the root's v2 frontend subagent was rejected; the coordinator relayed the exact batch/item and omitted-true contract instead. No tool rejection was bypassed.
- Remaining follow-ups: Acceptance independently reviews e7f4985 and checks old/new default serialization; data proves `GetDocumentV2`, `ListDocumentPageV2`, `CreateDocumentV2`, `SaveDocumentV2`, V3 payload/column consistency and all-save no-downgrade rules through the actual connector. Integrate Flutter's separate explicit selection/resume changes and verify the combined HTTP/UI path. Data's newly accessible actual object inventory does not establish source sensitivity: retain that factual gate before importing or materializing an explicitly false launch. Preserve the user's ten-source human-review scope, one execution and shared USD 5 ceiling; automatic classification/clearance remain deferred. No cloud query/mutation, paid call, real image inference, administrator elevation, source reclassification, push, merge, archive or pruning occurred in this runtime continuation.

Bound calculations to existing private live SQL/bucket/service metadata and independently summed the frozen metadata's ten originals to 3,018,274 bytes. No original bytes were read. Exact Decimal arithmetic gives a two-hour same-capacity restore clone at USD0.02738356 and a conservative full-capacity 30-day backup plus clone at USD0.87165556. Fixed infrastructure USD2.89 plus USD0.2055/region permits five regions inside the USD4 target or ten inside USD5; counts are illustrative ceilings, not permission to drop regions. Conditional smaller measured-size examples are provided privately and are explicitly not measured facts.

Current SAM startup hashes the mounted checkpoint before loading weights, creating at least two potential cross-region reads per cold start. An upload generation bound does not bound reads. Standard on-demand backup retention is indefinite; automatic retained-backup count seven is not seven-day expiry. Disabled secret versions still bill. App Check's empty configuration and disabled reCAPTCHA key API are not proof of unused assessment allowance; the first monthly threshold crossing may incur USD8. Cloud Run max instances is not an absolute cost cap. Carry retained and unknown liability across days without reset.

Docker display sizes differed from inspect Size values; neither was mislabeled as actual final registry billing. Earlier provisional infrastructure USD2.88 was refined to USD2.89 to include the full seven-day GCS soft-delete tail.
- Follow-ups: Same-worker all-ten segmentation/measurement then all-region admission and narrow test-backup cleanup after proof plus seven days were pending user decisions when this proposal was written. No assumed approval or cleanup was applied. Root must settle those decisions, verify reCAPTCHA billing basis, native timing, payload/region quote, final storage/backup sizes and all shared ledger liabilities before admission. ### 2026-09-09 UTC — Independent PG18 first-database role proposal review

- Task: delegated `/root/frontend_sensitive_intake`, coordinator `01a082b2-c2c3-70d2-be90-7bfb622c9102`; canonical `V0.1` at `/Users/anuragduddu/code-projects/fieldmuseum/specimen-digitization-app`, observed HEAD `2a787a95893605add3b2cb9dd53b9c43abfd6d25`. Bounded read-only review of the delivery session's unexecuted private first-database initialization proposal. This entry is the only repository edit by this review; other sessions' existing log/release-plan changes were preserved. No SQL connection, SQL execution, cloud request, permission grant, product edit, test execution, commit, PR, or deployment occurred.
- Durable evidence: `/Users/anuragduddu/.codex/rollout-state/specimen-live/first-database-initialization-20260908/proposed-role-transaction.sql` SHA256 `35221e3a1fb857873042bbbb2c7f2253180f4d8b1c2e104efcd53ceb44e5e302`; `RESEARCH.md` SHA256 `e89e5721830b847abf2a218db3a31bf7efa5704ad89ed9b8f3fe987a4419fd57`. Both were read completely and rehashed unchanged. Read current primary PostgreSQL 18 documentation for role attributes/membership, GRANT, SET ROLE, ALTER SCHEMA, defaults, privilege checks, pg_auth_members, DROP ROLE, LIKE and pg_type/CREATE TYPE. Static/documentation review only; native execution remains Not confirmed.
- Outcome: not ready to execute as written. Confirmed the coordinator's prefix finding: all three `nspname NOT LIKE 'pg_%'` guards exclude valid user schema `pgx`, because LIKE underscore is a one-character wildcard. Require a literal `pg_` prefix comparison and regressions for a pgx schema/object. Even after this repair, the pg_class/pg_namespace/pg_proc inventory misses a user enum/domain in public; PG18 pg_type records these without a pg_class relation. Add a public enum counterexample and explicit complete user-object inventory for the protected empty-database claim. These are documentation-based counterexamples, not locally executed reproductions. Sources: https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/functions-matching.html and https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/catalog-pg-type.html .
- Minimum permissions: proposed table defaults include TRUNCATE, which is a separate destructive privilege. No approved connector/runtime necessity is established by these artifacts; omit it unless the exact generated/native operation proves a need. Owner/writer separation also requires transitive membership, ADMIN/INHERIT/SET and effective-privilege checks; safe role attribute flags alone do not exclude an existing service-agent path through another login to the future owner or managed role. Fixed postconditions are presently comments immediately before COMMIT. The implementation must perform them within the transaction before committing, including effective ACLs/defaults, no upward privilege path, unchanged source-object inventory, and no initializer dependencies. Source: https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/ddl-priv.html and https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/functions-info.html .
- PG18-consistent design, conditional on native capability: SET LOCAL ROLE makes the permanent managed role the current creator; a nonsuperuser CREATEROLE creator receives the automatic bootstrap-superuser ADMIN grant. This keeps that dependency on the permanent role rather than temporary session_user. Explicit self-grants with INHERIT/SET are allowed and may coexist with bootstrap grantor rows; do not impose a false one-row-per-role/member invariant or revoke permanent ADMIN dependencies. Membership direction from app roles down to the permanent administrator is appropriate; never grant the managed role upward to app actors. Sources: https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/role-attributes.html and https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-grant.html .
- Schema/default-ACL requirements: temporarily granting database CREATE to the new owner, transferring public schema ownership, and revoking database CREATE follows ALTER SCHEMA requirements if the executing permanent role actually owns that schema and can SET the target role. Verify effective database CREATE is false after revocation, including other membership/PUBLIC paths. Owner-scoped defaults apply only to future objects created with current_user equal to owner; an ordinary maintenance login merely inheriting owner rights does not inherit those defaults. Later fixed DDL must SET the owner or prove actual native migration ownership/ACL behavior. Sources: https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-alterschema.html and https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-alterdefaultprivileges.html .
- Cleanup and unresolved follow-ups: inspect temporary-principal ownership, ACL/default-ACL and grantor dependencies across the cluster, because DROP ROLE can be blocked by references in any database; close initializer SQL sessions before revoking login/IAM access. Stop on unexpected dependency instead of using broad DROP OWNED, REASSIGN OWNED or CASCADE. Actual managed Cloud SQL CREATEROLE/SET behavior, API-created database/public-schema ownership, SQL Connect effective migration actor, role replacement/removal and principal deletion are Not confirmed. Qualify these on the already-approved single restore clone before any source initialization. No temporary SQL privilege was granted or admitted by this review. Sources: https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-droprole.html and https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-set-role.html . Findings were sent to root for runtime implementation and independent rereview; the cumulative USD5 and exact privilege/window authorization gates remain unchanged.

- Problem and result: root found raw native role/membership/ACL catalog observations could enter publicly uploaded data-ready artifacts. Added a pure in-memory encryption helper so the integration can publish ciphertext and bounded metadata. The runtime session owns the plan, inspect/absence/capability and failure-output integration, workflow publication allowlist and attestation binding. That integration and final frozen release acceptance are Not confirmed by this helper-only work.
- Cryptographic contract: existing uv.lock and installed cryptography are both 50.0.1; no dependency change. Require coordinator-provided canonical SubjectPublicKeyInfo public PEM (at most4096bytes), exact reviewed SHA256, RSA3072..8192 and exponent65537. Encrypt exact nonempty catalog bytes up to4MiB with a random256-bit AESGCM key and random12-byte nonce; wrap the AES key with RSA-OAEP using SHA256/MGF1-SHA256. Canonical authenticated metadata includes fixed version/algorithm, recipient SHA, plaintext SHA/size, fixed repository and exact source/run/attempt provenance. Exact key sets and strict numeric/encoding bounds reject unknown fields, malformed data and unsupported choices. No algorithm fallback, filesystem side effect, raw-data logging or key generation occurs in the helper. Official primitive references: https://cryptography.io/en/latest/hazmat/primitives/asymmetric/rsa/ and https://cryptography.io/en/latest/hazmat/primitives/aead/ .
- Agreed API: validate_public_key(public_key_pem:bytes, public_key_sha256:str); encrypt_catalog(catalog_bytes:bytes, public_key_pem:bytes, *, public_key_sha256:str, provenance:dict)->dict; decrypt_catalog(envelope:dict, private_key_pem:bytes, *, public_key_sha256:str, provenance:dict)->bytes. Plan recipient is exactly public_key_pem/public_key_sha256; provenance is exactly repository/source_sha/run_id/run_attempt. Private-key decryption is coordinator/local-only and rejected when GITHUB_ACTIONS=true. The public runner receives only the recipient public key. The caller must bound/strictly parse artifact JSON and verify protected-workflow attestation: encryption provides confidentiality and tamper detection but does not prove producer identity.
- Actual TDD: initial focused test failed because the envelope helper did not exist; after implementation the focused suite passed52 in5.11s. Tests include exact-byte round trip, an independent decrypt using standard OAEP/AESGCM primitives rather than the helper, fresh randomness, key/nonce/ciphertext and metadata tampering, wrong key, expected and rebinding provenance failures, unknown/missing fields, strict int/base64/size limits, maximum4MiB round trip, RSA3072/4096 acceptance, RSA2048/EC/legacy-exponent rejection, canonical PEM/pin enforcement, public/error-output canary absence, no caller metadata mutation and runner decryption denial. Syntax compilation and whitespace checks passed. No full product suite or live artifact upload was run by this bounded task.
